<!-- /greptile_comment -->25:Tb29,Reopened from #6379 (was merged then rolled back)

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds defensive validation to the `ensureDir` function in `packages/cli/src/utils/get-config.ts` to prevent ENOENT errors when directory paths are empty or contain only whitespace.

**Key Changes:**
- Added guard clause checking if `dirPath` is empty or contains only whitespace
- Throws clear error message: "Directory path cannot be empty" instead of allowing confusing ENOENT errors from `fs.mkdir`

**Root Cause:**
When environment variables like `PGLITE_DATA_DIR` are set to empty strings (as opposed to being undefined), the nullish coalescing operator (`??`) in `resolvePgliteDir` doesn't skip them, leading to empty paths being passed to `ensureDir`.

**Impact:**
This is a defensive programming fix that improves error messages and prevents runtime crashes. The change is minimal, safe, and directly addresses the issue described in the original PR #6379.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk
- The change adds a simple validation check that prevents empty directory paths from causing ENOENT errors. It's a defensive programming practice with clear benefits: better error messages, no breaking changes to existing functionality, and addresses a real edge case where environment variables might be set to empty strings. The fix is minimal (3 lines), well-scoped, and doesn't alter any logic paths for valid inputs.
- No files require special attention

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/cli/src/utils/get-config.ts | Added validation to prevent empty directory paths in `ensureDir`, fixing ENOENT errors when environment variables are set to empty strings |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant CLI
    participant getConfig as get-config.ts
    participant ensureDir
    participant fs as Node.js fs

    User->>CLI: Start CLI with empty PGLITE_DATA_DIR env var
    CLI->>getConfig: configureDatabaseSettings()
    getConfig->>getConfig: resolvePgliteDir()
    Note over getConfig: env var = "" (empty string)<br/>fallbackDir is ignored
    getConfig->>getConfig: Returns empty string
    getConfig->>ensureDir: ensureDir("")
    
    alt With PR Fix
        ensureDir->>ensureDir: Check if dirPath is empty/whitespace
        ensureDir-->>getConfig: throw Error("Directory path cannot be empty")
        Note over getConfig: Clear error message
    else Without PR Fix
        ensureDir->>fs: mkdir("", {recursive: true})
        fs-->>ensureDir: ENOENT error
        Note over ensureDir: Confusing error message
    end
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2e:Tc65,## Summary

- **Fix**: Add null check for `runtime.providers` in `providersProvider`
- **Impact**: Prevents `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'filter')`

## Problem

When `runtime.providers` is `null` or `undefined`, the code crashes when attempting to call `.filter()` on it:

```typescript
const allProviders = runtime.providers;  // Can be null/undefined
const dynamicProviders = allProviders.filter(...);  // CRASHES
```

## Solution

Added defensive null check:

```typescript
const allProviders = runtime.providers || [];  // Safe fallback
const dynamicProviders = allProviders.filter(...);  // Safe
```

## Testing

- ✅ Verified fix prevents crash when providers is null/undefined
- ✅ Verified normal operation when providers array exists

## Files Changed

- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/providers.ts` - Line 23

---

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

- Adds a defensive fallback when reading `runtime.providers` in plugin-bootstrap’s `providersProvider` to avoid calling `.filter()` on `null`/`undefined`.
- Keeps existing provider filtering behavior by treating missing provider lists as an empty array.
- Change is localized to `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/providers.ts` and affects how bootstrap providers are enumerated at runtime.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk.
- Change is a small, localized defensive fix that prevents a concrete runtime crash when `runtime.providers` is null/undefined, without altering behavior when providers are present.
- No files require special attention

### Description
ProofGate provides blockchain transaction guardrails for AI agents:
- Validate transactions before execution
- Auto-block unsafe transactions (infinite approvals, drainer contracts, etc.)

- Custom guardrail policies

- `PROOFGATE_VALIDATE_TRANSACTION` - Full validation with detailed response
- `PROOFGATE_CHECK_SAFETY` - Quick boolean safety check
- `PROOFGATE_GET_EVIDENCE` - Retrieve on-chain proof

### Installation
```bash
npm install @proofgate/eliza-plugin
```

### Configuration
Requires `PROOFGATE_API_KEY` environment variable.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->31:T1fa8,Today, ElizaOS significantly advanced its core capabilities with a comprehensive JWT authentication and user management system, alongside major enhancements to its EVM and Solana plugins for improved reliability and expanded functionality. ## ✅ Completed Work
### Core Platform Enhancements & Agent Intelligence
- **elizaos/eliza**: Implemented a comprehensive JWT authentication and user management system, including support for external JWT providers and data isolation, significantly bolstering security and multi-entity support ([elizaos/eliza#6200](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6200)).
- **elizaos/eliza**: Optimized the `plugin-bootstrap` package with performance enhancements, caching, and type safety ([elizaos/eliza#6476](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6476)).
- **elizaos/eliza**: Introduced a new `ActionFilterService` utilizing vector search and BM25 reranking to dynamically filter actions for the LLM, reducing prompt bloat and improving relevance ([elizaos/eliza#6475](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6475)).
- **elizaos/eliza**: Added null checks to `Object.entries` calls in settings utilities within `@elizaos/core` and for `runtime.providers` in `plugin-bootstrap` to prevent runtime errors ([elizaos/eliza#6473](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6473), [elizaos/eliza#6471](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6471)).

TODO
- LLM mode -- can be SMALL, LARGE or DEFAULT -- SMALL and LARGE override the LLM small or large so all use the small or all use the large
- checkShouldRespond defaults to true but can be turned off for ChatGPT mode3d:T1cd9,## Summary

Fixes critical runtime errors in `plugin-bootstrap` providers when metadata or values are null/undefined.

## Problem

The agent crashes with the error:
```
Object.entries requires that input parameter not be null or undefined
```

This occurs in several providers:
1. **relationshipsProvider**: When `entity.metadata` is null/undefined
2. **actionStateProvider**: When `result.values` or `workingMemory` are not objects
3. **recentMessagesProvider**: Similar issues with null values

## Solution

Added proper null/undefined checks before calling `Object.entries()`:

### 1. `src/providers/relationships.ts`
- Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()`
- Returns `'{}'` for null/undefined metadata instead of crashing

### 2. `src/providers/actionState.ts`  
- Added type check for `result.values` before calling `Object.entries()`
- Added type/null check for `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()`

## Changes

- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts**: Guard `formatMetadata()` against null metadata
- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts**: Add type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory`

## Testing

1. Started ElizaOS agent
2. Sent messages via web interface (http://localhost:3000)
3. Verified no more `Object.entries` errors
4. Confirmed agent responds properly instead of showing IGNORE action

## Impact

- ✅ Prevents agent crashes when entities have null metadata
- ✅ Improves stability for action result processing
- ✅ Fixes "IGNORE" action issue when agent can't retrieve conversation context
- ✅ No breaking changes - only adds safety checks

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes critical `Object.entries` runtime errors in plugin-bootstrap providers that caused agent crashes when metadata or values were null/undefined. However, **the PR contains significantly more changes than described in the title and description**.

## What's Actually in This PR

### 1. Plugin-Bootstrap Fixes (Matches PR Description)
- `actionState.ts`: Added type guards before `Object.entries()` on `result.values` and `workingMemory`
- `relationships.ts`: Added null check in `formatMetadata()` to prevent crashes

### 2. Major New Feature (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- **Request Context System**: New per-entity settings infrastructure for multi-tenant deployments
  - Added `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` and `request-context.node.ts` (856+ lines)
  - Modified `runtime.ts` `getSetting()` to check request context first
  - Enables different users sharing the same runtime to have different API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.

### 3. Message Service Changes (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- Refactored message creation logic in `message.ts`
- Added `MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after sending to central server

### 4. Version Bumps
- All packages bumped to `1.7.3-alpha.3`

## Concerns

The PR title says "fix: Add null/undefined checks" but this PR includes:
- A major architectural feature (request context system)
- Message service refactoring
- 30 files changed, 1107 insertions, 52 deletions

**This should have been split into separate PRs** for better review, testing, and rollback capability. The plugin-bootstrap fixes are straightforward and safe, but bundling them with a major new feature makes it difficult to:
- Review each change independently
- Test each feature in isolation
- Roll back if issues arise with one component

## Technical Review

The actual code changes are well-implemented:
- Null checks are correctly placed and handle edge cases
- Request context system follows AsyncLocalStorage patterns appropriately
- Message service changes maintain event emission order

The plugin-bootstrap fixes will definitely prevent the `Object.entries` crashes described in the PR.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

- This PR contains well-implemented code but has significant scope creep beyond its stated purpose
- Score of 3 reflects that while the code quality is good and the plugin-bootstrap fixes are safe, the PR includes undocumented major features (request context system, message service changes) that should have been separate PRs. This makes comprehensive testing difficult and increases risk. The PR description is misleading about the actual scope of changes.
- Pay close attention to `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` and `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` as these introduce a new architectural pattern for per-entity settings that affects how settings are resolved throughout the system

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts | Added type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()` to prevent runtime errors when these values are null/undefined |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts | Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()` to return `'{}'` when metadata is null/undefined instead of crashing on `Object.entries()` |
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | Added request context lookup in `getSetting()` for per-entity settings support - enables multi-tenant deployments with per-user API keys |
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Refactored message creation to emit MESSAGE_SENT event after successfully sending to central server, improving event lifecycle tracking |
| packages/core/src/request-context.ts | New file implementing request context system for per-entity settings in multi-tenant deployments |

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant MessageService
    participant Runtime
    participant Provider
    participant Database

    Note over User,Database: Object.entries Error Flow (Before Fix)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Access result.values (null)
    Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(null) ❌
    Provider-->>Runtime: CRASH

    Note over User,Database: Fixed Flow (After This PR)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Check if result.values is object
    alt result.values is null/undefined
        Provider->>Provider: Skip Object.entries
    else result.values is valid object
        Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(result.values) ✓
    end
    Provider-->>Runtime: Return formatted context
    Runtime->>Runtime: Generate response
    Runtime->>Database: Create memory
    MessageService->>MessageService: Emit MESSAGE_SENT event
    MessageService-->>User: Response delivered

    Note over User,Database: Request Context Feature (New)
    User->>Runtime: getSetting(key)
    Runtime->>Runtime: Check request context
    alt Entity-specific setting exists
        Runtime-->>User: Return entity setting
    else No entity setting
        Runtime->>Runtime: Fall back to agent setting
        Runtime-->>User: Return agent setting
    end
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->3e:T17a7,## Relates to

- Data isolation / multi-entity support
- External JWT provider integration (Privy, Auth0, Clerk, Supabase, Google, Embbeded)

## Risks

**Low**

- Requires `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=true` to activate JWT auth mode

## Background

### What does this PR do?

Implements a complete JWT authentication system for ElizaOS with support for multiple verification strategies:

1. **JWT Verifier Factory** - Priority-based verifier selection:
   - Ed25519 (highest priority) - For self-signed JWTs with Ed25519 keys
   - JWKS (medium priority) - For external providers (Auth0, Clerk, Privy, Supabase, Google)
   - Secret (lowest priority) - For simple HS256 symmetric key verification

2. **Entity ID derivation from JWT `sub` claim**:
   - `entityId = stringToUuid(payload.sub)`
   - Compatible with ANY JWT provider since `sub` is a standard claim
   - No custom claims needed in external JWTs

3. **Dual authentication modes**:
   - `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=true` → JWT authentication required
   - `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=false` → X-Entity-Id header (legacy mode)

4. **Internal service bypass** - Process-local UUID secret for service-to-service calls

5. **Credentials-based auth endpoints** (`/api/auth/*`) - only in custom - JWT-SECRET mode:
   - `POST /register` - User registration with bcrypt password hashing
   - `POST /login` - User login with JWT generation
   - `POST /refresh` - Token refresh
   - `GET /me` - Current user info

6. **Client UI default implementation using self mode (Secret)

### What kind of change is this?

**Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

## Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    JWT Verification Flow                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Request with Bearer token                                       │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  JWTVerifierFactory.create()                                     │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  Priority: Ed25519 > JWKS > Secret > Disabled                   │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  verifier.verify(token)                                          │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  Extract payload.sub                                             │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  entityId = stringToUuid(sub)  ← Derived, NOT stored in JWT     │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  req.entityId = entityId                                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Environment Variables

| Variable | Description | Example |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION` | Enable JWT auth mode | `true` |
| `JWT_SECRET` (optional) | HS256 symmetric secret | `your-secret-key` |
| `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_ED25519` (optional) | Ed25519 public key (base64) | `MCowBQYDK2Vw...` |
| `JWT_JWKS_URI` (optional) | JWKS endpoint URL | `https://auth0.com/.well-known/jwks.json` |
| `JWT_ISSUER_WHITELIST` (optional) | Allowed issuers (comma-separated) | `https://auth0.com/,https://clerk.dev` |

## Documentation changes needed?

Yes - Documentation should be added for:
- Environment variable configuration
- External provider setup (Auth0, Clerk, Privy examples)

## Testing

### Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/server/src/services/jwt-verifiers/factory.ts` - Core factory logic
2. `packages/server/src/middleware/jwtMiddleware.ts` - HTTP middleware
3. `packages/server/src/socketio/index.ts` - SocketIO authentication

### Detailed testing steps

**Unit Tests:**
```bash
cd packages/server
bun run test:unit
```

**Integration Tests:**
```bash
cd packages/server
bun run test:integration
```

**Results:** 489 unit tests pass, 6 integration test files pass

### Manual Testing

1. **Test with JWT_SECRET (HS256):**
```bash
ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=true JWT_SECRET=test-secret bun run dev
```

2. **Register a user:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/register \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"email":"test@example.com","username":"testuser","password":"password123"}'
```

3. **Use returned token:**
```bash
curl http://localhost:3000/api/agents \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer <token>"
```

## External Provider Compatibility

Tested JWT formats:
- **Auth0**: `sub: "auth0|1234567890"`
- **Clerk**: `sub: "user_2abcdefgh123456"`
- **Google**: `sub: "1234567890"` with `email`, `email_verified`
- **Supabase**: `sub: "uuid"` with `role: "authenticated"`
- **Privy**: `sub: "did:privy:..."`

All providers work because we only require the standard `sub` claim.

## Next Idea Steps: Access Control Layer (ACL)

The next phase will add access control on agents and rooms: 

Agents:
- Each user can create their own agents (ownerId = entityId)
- Visibility: public or private
- Private = only the owner can chat with it
- Public = everyone can chat, but only the owner can modify/delete

Rooms:
- Each room has an owner
Only the owner can:
- Delete the room (DELETE /rooms/:id)
- Clear message history (DELETE /rooms/:id/messages)
- Change visibility

This will enable true multi-tenant mode where each user manages their resources in isolation.3f:T2345,Reopened from #6200 (was merged then rolled back)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR implements comprehensive JWT authentication and user management for ElizaOS, adding support for multiple authentication providers (Ed25519/Privy, JWKS, and custom secret-based auth).

## Key Changes

- **Universal JWT Verifier**: Pluggable architecture supporting Ed25519, JWKS (Auth0/Clerk/Supabase), and shared secret verification with configurable priority
- **Dual Authentication Modes**: Legacy mode (X-Entity-Id header) vs secure mode (JWT with data isolation) controlled by `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION` flag
- **User Management**: Complete registration/login/refresh endpoints with bcrypt password hashing, rate limiting (10 attempts/15min), and database schema
- **WebSocket Security**: JWT authentication in handshake middleware with channel access control and permission verification
- **Client Integration**: React authentication context with token persistence, auto-reconnection, and auth dialog with form validation
- **Database Layer**: Added user management methods to IDatabaseAdapter and BaseDrizzleAdapter with case-insensitive email queries
- **Comprehensive Testing**: 476-line integration test suite covering all JWT providers, issuer whitelists, and entity ID consistency

## Issues Found

1. **Inconsistent JWT Verification**: `/auth/refresh` and `/auth/me` endpoints bypass the universal JWT verifier and hardcode secret-based verification, breaking compatibility with Ed25519/JWKS providers and skipping issuer whitelist validation
2. **Limited Token Generation**: `generateAuthToken` only supports `JWT_SECRET` mode and cannot generate tokens for external providers, making refresh tokens incompatible with multi-provider setups
3. **Type Safety**: Database user methods use `any` instead of `User` type in core/database.ts (fixed in plugin-sql but not in abstract class)
4. **Breaking Change**: `getLogs` changed `entityId` from optional to required without migration guide

## Security Posture

The implementation includes strong security foundations: rate limiting on auth endpoints, bcrypt with cost factor 10, internal service authentication with runtime-generated secrets, proper disconnection guards in WebSocket handling, and issuer whitelist validation. However, the inconsistent JWT verification in refresh endpoints creates a security gap where tokens from external providers cannot be properly validated.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

- Safe to merge with issues that need addressing in follow-up
- Score reflects solid architecture and comprehensive testing but with critical logic issues in auth/credentials.ts that break multi-provider JWT support. The core authentication infrastructure is well-designed with proper rate limiting and security measures, but the hardcoded JWT verification in refresh endpoints bypasses the universal verifier, making those endpoints incompatible with Ed25519/JWKS providers. These issues don't introduce security vulnerabilities but will cause runtime failures when using non-secret authentication providers.
- packages/server/src/api/auth/credentials.ts (refresh and me endpoints need to use universal JWT verifier), packages/core/src/database.ts (type signatures should use User instead of any)

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/server/src/api/auth/credentials.ts | Implements custom credential-based authentication with registration, login, refresh, and user info endpoints. Uses bcrypt for password hashing and JWT_SECRET for token signing. Contains hardcoded JWT verification logic in refresh/me endpoints that doesn't use the universal JWT verifier. |
| packages/server/src/middleware/entity-auth.ts | Implements dual-mode authentication middleware (legacy header-based vs JWT-based). Includes internal service authentication using runtime-generated secret. Properly handles data isolation mode with JWT verification and entityId extraction. |
| packages/server/src/services/jwt-verifier.ts | Universal JWT verifier factory that creates appropriate verifier (Ed25519, JWKS, or Secret) based on environment configuration. Clean architecture with singleton pattern and backwards compatibility. |
| packages/server/src/socketio/index.ts | Implements WebSocket authentication with JWT verification in handshake middleware. Includes channel access control, permission verification, and proper disconnection guards. Handles both legacy and data isolation modes. |
| packages/client/src/context/AuthContext.tsx | Client-side authentication context managing JWT tokens, login/logout, and socket reconnection. Handles token persistence in localStorage with origin-specific keys. Auto-opens auth dialog when server requires authentication. |
| packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts | Added user management methods to database adapter (getUserByEmail, getUserByUsername, getUserById, createUser, updateUserLastLogin). Email queries use toLowerCase() for case-insensitive matching. |
| packages/server/src/middleware/rate-limit.ts | Comprehensive rate limiting for auth endpoints (10 attempts per 15 min), API endpoints (1000 per 15 min), uploads (50 per 15 min), and channel validation (200 per 10 min). Includes skipSuccessfulRequests for auth to prevent legitimate logins from counting. |
| packages/core/src/types/database.ts | Added user management methods to IDatabaseAdapter interface (getUserByEmail, getUserByUsername, getUserById, createUser, updateUserLastLogin). Changed getLogs entityId parameter from optional to required. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant AuthDialog
    participant API as Express API
    participant AuthMiddleware as Entity Auth Middleware
    participant JWTVerifier as Universal JWT Verifier
    participant DB as Database
    participant Socket as Socket.IO Server
    participant AgentRuntime as Agent Runtime

    Note over Client,AgentRuntime: User Registration Flow
    Client->>AuthDialog: Open auth dialog
    AuthDialog->>API: POST /api/auth/register<br/>{email, username, password}
    API->>API: Validate input (email format, password length ≥8)
    API->>DB: Check if email/username exists
    DB-->>API: User not found
    API->>API: Hash password with bcrypt (cost=10)
    API->>DB: Create user with UUID
    DB-->>API: User created
    API->>API: Generate JWT (HS256, 7d expiry)
    API-->>AuthDialog: {token, entityId, username}
    AuthDialog->>Client: Store token in localStorage
    Client->>Client: Update API client config with JWT

    Note over Client,AgentRuntime: HTTP Request with JWT
    Client->>API: GET /api/agents (with Bearer token)
    API->>AuthMiddleware: Authenticate request
    AuthMiddleware->>AuthMiddleware: Check ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION flag
    alt Data Isolation Enabled
        AuthMiddleware->>JWTVerifier: verify(token)
        JWTVerifier->>JWTVerifier: Select verifier<br/>(Ed25519/JWKS/Secret)
        JWTVerifier->>JWTVerifier: Validate issuer whitelist
        JWTVerifier-->>AuthMiddleware: {entityId, sub, payload}
        AuthMiddleware->>AuthMiddleware: Set req.entityId
    else Legacy Mode
        AuthMiddleware->>AuthMiddleware: Extract X-Entity-Id header
    end
    AuthMiddleware->>API: Continue to route handler
    API-->>Client: Response data

    Note over Client,AgentRuntime: WebSocket Connection Flow
    Client->>Socket: Connect with auth token in handshake
    Socket->>Socket: Authentication middleware
    alt Data Isolation Enabled
        Socket->>JWTVerifier: verify(token)
        JWTVerifier-->>Socket: {entityId, sub, payload}
    else Legacy Mode
        Socket->>Socket: Use client-provided entityId
    end
    Socket->>Socket: Store entityId in socket.data
    Socket->>Socket: Track entity→sockets mapping
    Socket-->>Client: Emit 'authenticated' event

    Note over Client,AgentRuntime: Channel Join with Permission Check
    Client->>Socket: ROOM_JOINING {channelId, agentId}
    Socket->>DB: isChannelParticipant(channelId, entityId)
    DB-->>Socket: Permission result
    alt Permission Granted
        Socket->>Socket: Join socket room
        Socket->>AgentRuntime: Emit ENTITY_JOINED event
        Socket-->>Client: 'channel_joined' event
    else Permission Denied
        Socket-->>Client: Error: Access denied
    end

    Note over Client,AgentRuntime: Message Submission
    Client->>Socket: SEND_MESSAGE {channelId, message, senderId}
    Socket->>DB: verifyChannelAccess(channelId, entityId)
    Socket->>DB: Create message
    DB-->>Socket: Message created
    Socket->>Client: Broadcast message to channel
    Socket->>AgentRuntime: Emit to message bus for agent processing
    AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: Process message with agent
    AgentRuntime->>Socket: Stream response chunks
    Socket-->>Client: MESSAGE_STREAM_EVENT chunks
```

> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Changes core provider caching behavior by adding a process-wide sweep timer and altering eviction semantics; mistakes could cause unexpected cache churn or lingering timers in long-running runtimes/tests.
> 
> **Overview**
> **Fixes unbounded cache growth in `plugin-bootstrap`’s shared provider cache.** Adds a periodic TTL sweep (unref’d interval) that evicts expired entries across all in-memory caches, and replaces the old size-based `cleanupCache` with TTL-aware `evictExpired` used by both inline burst-guard eviction and the sweep.
> 
> Improves operational visibility/control by logging when `withTimeout` returns a fallback due to DB timeouts and exporting `stopCacheMaintenance` (via `providers/index.ts`) to stop the sweep and clear caches for shutdown/tests. Also expands `README.md` with a fuller overview and performance/architecture documentation.
> 
> <sup>Written by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot) for commit 9edd4249ed406a8805e054dcd41fdb03bfa1ac72. This will update automatically on new commits. Configure [here](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot).</sup>
<!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY -->

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR tightens the provider shared-cache behavior by evicting entries based on their actual TTL (including negative-cache TTL), adding a periodic sweep across all cache maps, and exporting a `stopCacheMaintenance()` helper intended for shutdown/test teardown. It also adds a warning log when DB operations exceed the configured timeout.

Key correctness concerns in the current implementation are: (1) the sweep `setInterval` is created at module import time without any wiring that guarantees `stopCacheMaintenance()` runs on teardown, and (2) the `withTimeout` logger can emit “timed out” even when the main operation succeeds because the timeout callback is not suppressed after the race resolves. The README update also links to a non-existent `OPTIMIZATION_GUIDE.md`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

- This PR has a few merge-blocking correctness issues despite addressing the intended memory leak.
- Score reduced due to a module-level periodic sweep interval that is not guaranteed to be stopped on teardown, and a timeout warning that can fire after successful operations, producing false alarms; README also contains a broken link.
- packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/shared-cache.ts and packages/plugin-bootstrap/README.md

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/shared-cache.ts | Adds TTL-based eviction + periodic sweep + stopCacheMaintenance; but introduces a module-level interval without teardown wiring and a timeout logger that can fire after successful operations. |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/index.ts | Re-exports stopCacheMaintenance from shared-cache; no functional changes beyond export surface. |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/entities.ts | Adds explanatory WHY comments; runtime behavior unchanged in this PR slice. |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/README.md | Large README rewrite describing optimizations/config; includes an OPTIMIZATION_GUIDE.md link that may not exist in-repo. |

</details>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant P as Provider (e.g., ENTITIES)
    participant SC as shared-cache.ts
    participant C as Cache Maps
    participant DB as runtime.get* (DB)

    P->>SC: getCachedRoom(roomId)
    SC->>C: roomCache.get(roomId)
    alt fresh cache hit
        C-->>SC: CacheEntry(room)
        SC-->>P: room
    else miss/stale
        SC->>C: roomInFlight.get(roomId)
        alt in-flight exists
            C-->>SC: Promise(room)
            SC-->>P: Promise(room)
        else start fetch
            SC->>C: roomInFlight.set(roomId, fetchPromise)
            SC->>DB: withTimeout(runtime.getRoom(roomId), 5000ms, null)
            alt DB resolves before timeout
                DB-->>SC: room
            else timeout fires
                SC-->>SC: logger.warn(timeout)
                SC-->>SC: resolve fallback (null)
            end
            SC->>C: roomCache.set(roomId, CacheEntry)
            SC->>C: roomInFlight.delete(roomId)
            SC-->>P: room|null
        end
    end

    Note over SC,C: Periodic maintenance (module-level)
    loop every 60s
        SC->>C: evictExpired() across all caches
    end
    SC-->>P: stopCacheMaintenance() clears interval + maps (if called)
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR introduces an `ActionFilterService` that reduces action prompt bloat by ranking actions for each message using a two-stage pipeline: vector similarity (cosine over embeddings) with BM25 keyword reranking. The runtime integrates the service by auto-indexing actions on `registerAction()`, tracking per-room momentum from completed actions, and reporting “filter misses” when the LLM selects an action that was previously filtered out. `actionsProvider` now uses the filter service when available (falling back to the existing validate-all path otherwise). Additionally, `composeState()` can auto-include relevant dynamic providers via a BM25 provider index, and `BaseRoute` gains an optional `x402` payment configuration field.

Key flow: bootstrap registers the service → service builds BM25/embedding indices → actions provider calls `filter()` before validation → runtime records usage and miss telemetry, and optionally BM25-selects dynamic providers during state composition.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

- This PR is close to mergeable but has a correctness issue in always-include handling that can change runtime behavior.
- Most changes are additive and well-tested, and fallbacks are designed to preserve prior behavior when the service/model is unavailable. However, `ActionFilterService.filter()` currently ignores the `Action.alwaysInclude` flag and instead checks only a configured name list and a tag, which will incorrectly allow actions intended to always appear to be filtered out.
- packages/typescript/src/services/action-filter.ts; packages/typescript/src/__tests__/action-filter.test.ts

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/typescript/src/services/action-filter.ts | Implements ActionFilterService (vector + BM25) and provider BM25 filtering; found always-include logic ignores `action.alwaysInclude` and uses tag-based fallback instead, likely breaking intended behavior and tests. |
| packages/typescript/src/services/bm25.ts | Adds dependency-free BM25 index with shared Tokenizer + unicode fallback tokenization; no clear functional issues found in scoring/add/remove logic. |
| packages/typescript/src/services/cosine-similarity.ts | Adds cosine similarity and vector normalization utilities with guardrails for NaN/Infinity/degenerate vectors; no issues found. |
| packages/typescript/src/bootstrap/providers/actions.ts | Updates actions provider to use ActionFilterService when available then validates actions; appears consistent with previous validate-all behavior on fallback. |
| packages/typescript/src/runtime.ts | Integrates ActionFilterService: indexes newly registered actions, records action-use momentum, reports filter misses, and BM25-includes dynamic providers in composeState; no definite runtime-breaking issues found. |
| packages/typescript/src/bootstrap/index.ts | Registers ActionFilterService in basic bootstrap services; straightforward wiring change. |
| packages/typescript/src/types/plugin.ts | Extends BaseRoute with optional x402 payment config; type-only change, no runtime impact. |
| packages/typescript/src/__tests__/action-filter.test.ts | Adds comprehensive tests for BM25/cosine/action filtering; note tests assume per-action `alwaysInclude` is honored, which current service implementation does not. |

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  autonumber
  participant RT as AgentRuntime
  participant BSP as BootstrapPlugin
  participant AFS as ActionFilterService
  participant AP as actionsProvider
  participant BM as BM25Index
  participant EM as EmbeddingModel
  participant LLM as LLM prompt composer

  BSP->>RT: registerService(ActionFilterService)
  RT->>AFS: start(runtime)
  AFS->>RT: getModel(TEXT_EMBEDDING)
  alt embedding available
    AFS->>EM: useModel(TEXT_EMBEDDING, text=actionIndexText)
    AFS->>AFS: store actionEmbeddings
  else no embedding
    AFS->>AFS: BM25-only mode
  end
  AFS->>BM: addDocument(action.name, actionText)
  AFS->>BM: addDocument(provider.name, providerText)

  Note over RT,AP: On each message
  RT->>AP: get(runtime,message,state)
  AP->>RT: getService("action_filter")
  alt filter service present
    AP->>AFS: filter(runtime,message,state)
    AFS->>AFS: buildQueryText(message,state)
    alt vector stage possible
      AFS->>EM: useModel(TEXT_EMBEDDING, text=queryText)
      AFS->>AFS: cosineSimilarity(queryEmb, actionEmb)
      AFS->>AFS: pick top vectorTopK candidates
    end
    AFS->>BM: searchSubset(queryText, candidateIds)
    AFS->>AFS: combine scores + momentum
    AFS-->>AP: top actions
  else no service
    AP->>AP: validateActions(runtime.actions)
  end
  AP->>LLM: provide filtered actions section

  Note over RT,AFS: Provider filtering during composeState
  RT->>AFS: filterProviders(dynamicProviders,message,cachedState)
  AFS->>BM: searchSubset(queryText, providerIds)
  AFS-->>RT: relevant provider names
```

This commit merges critical performance optimizations, caching improvements, and robustness enhancements while preserving type safety improvements from upstream.

## New Features
- Added plugin initialization banner with configuration display
- Added bootstrap plugin self-documentation providers (instructions & settings)
- Implemented comprehensive two-level caching system (agent-specific + cross-agent) with TTL, in-flight promise tracking, and timeout protection

## Provider Optimizations
- **entities**: O(1) entity lookups using Map, optimized component merging
- **recentMessages**: Cross-provider cache reuse, LIMIT_TO_LAST_MESSAGE setting, conditional formatting (only formats what's needed), entity map for O(1) lookups
- **roles**: Shared cache for room/world lookups
- **settings**: Shared cache with timeout protection, getCachedSettingsByServerId
- **character**: Uses getCachedRoom()
- **evaluators**: Null-safety for examples/outcome, try/catch on validation
- **attachments**: Data URL summarization (prevents dumping base64 blobs into context)
- **actions**: Structured logger instead of console.error, early return optimization
- **anxiety**: Added 3 critical anti-loop examples to prevent endless acknowledgments
- **relationships**: Token-efficient CSV format instead of verbose JSON metadata
- **actionState**: Added dynamic: true flag
- **choice**: Added dynamic: true flag

## Evaluator Performance Improvements
- **reflection**: Restored O(1) Map-based entity/relationship lookups (was O(n) find())
- **reflection**: Restored parallel Promise.all() processing for relationships
- **reflection**: Restored state cache optimization for entity fetching

## Core Improvements
- Enhanced XML parsing with proper generic type parameters (parseKeyValueXml<T>)
- Added inline TypeScript interfaces for better type safety
- Memory creation controls (DISABLE_MEMORY_CREATION, ALLOW_MEMORY_SOURCE_IDS)
- Proper ControlMessagePayload usage
- Action notification handlers (ACTION_STARTED, ACTION_COMPLETED)
- Enhanced data URL handling in fetchMediaData (supports data: URI scheme)

## Files Changed

- New: 3 files (banner.ts, plugin-info.ts, shared-cache.ts)

## Performance Impact
- Reduced redundant database calls via shared caching
- Prevented duplicate concurrent requests via in-flight promise tracking
- O(1) instead of O(n) entity/relationship lookups in reflection evaluator
- Parallel instead of sequential relationship processing
- Conditional formatting saves unnecessary computation
- Base64 data URL summarization saves massive token usage

Builds successfully. Tests pass (26/29, 3 pre-existing test infrastructure issues).

> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Touches many bootstrap providers/evaluators and changes caching and memory-ingest behavior, which could affect runtime context generation and DB load; mitigated by mostly additive changes and conservative fallbacks/timeouts.
> 
> **Overview**
> **Bootstrap plugin runtime is optimized and hardened** by adding a shared two-level cache (agent + cross-agent) with TTL, in-flight promise coalescing, timeouts, and negative caching, then refactoring multiple providers/evaluators to reuse cached room/world/entities and avoid redundant DB calls and formatting.
> 
> Adds a startup `printBanner` (ASCII + settings status) and two self-documentation providers (`bootstrapInstructions`, `bootstrapSettings`). Tightens behavior and token usage: new memory-ingest controls (`DISABLE_MEMORY_CREATION`, `ALLOW_MEMORY_SOURCE_IDS`), support for `data:` URLs and summarizing them in attachment context, CSV formatting for relationships, anti-acknowledgment-loop guidance, and reflection evaluator speedups (map-based entity/relationship lookups + parallel relationship writes + better validation/caching).
>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR implements comprehensive performance optimizations and robustness improvements for the bootstrap plugin, focusing on reducing database calls and improving token efficiency.

## Key Changes

**Caching Infrastructure (shared-cache.ts)**
- Introduces two-level caching system: agent-specific (by roomId/worldId) + cross-agent (by external Discord IDs)
- Promise coalescing prevents duplicate concurrent requests (thundering herd protection)
- 5-second timeout protection prevents 80+ second DB waits
- Negative caching for worlds without settings reduces futile lookups
- 30-second TTL with automatic cleanup at 500/200 entry thresholds

**Reflection Evaluator Optimizations**
- Restored O(1) Map-based entity lookups (was O(n) find operations)
- Parallel Promise.all() for relationship processing instead of sequential awaits
- Reuses entity data from state cache when ENTITIES provider already ran
- Build entity lookup maps once, use for all resolutions

**Provider Optimizations**
- **entities**: O(1) Map lookups, efficient component merging with deduplication
- **recentMessages**: Cross-provider cache reuse, LIMIT_TO_LAST_MESSAGE setting, conditional formatting (only formats posts OR messages, not both), entity Maps for O(1) lookups
- **settings**: Integrated shared cache with cross-agent optimization by raw serverId
- **attachments**: Data URL summarization prevents dumping base64 blobs into context (massive token savings)
- **anxiety**: Added 3 critical anti-loop examples to prevent endless acknowledgment ping-pong
- **relationships**: CSV format instead of verbose JSON for metadata (token efficient)
- **actions**: Replaced console.error with structured logger
- **roles/character**: Use getCachedRoom() to avoid redundant DB calls

**Core Features (index.ts)**
- Memory creation controls: DISABLE_MEMORY_CREATION and ALLOW_MEMORY_SOURCE_IDS settings
- Data URL (data:image/png;base64,...) support in fetchMediaData
- Action notification handlers (ACTION_STARTED, ACTION_COMPLETED)
- Improved null checks and error handling

**New Features**
- Banner with ASCII art showing plugin configuration (banner.ts)
- Self-documentation providers for bootstrap instructions and settings (plugin-info.ts)
- Dynamic flags added to actionState and choice providers

## Issues Found

- ANSI color codes in banner.ts are incorrect (teal=red, should be cyan)
- Type safety reduced by replacing TypeScript interfaces with `any[]` in reflection evaluator
- parseKeyValueXml calls missing generic type parameters despite PR claiming "proper generic type parameters"

## Performance Impact

Significant improvements expected:
- Reduced redundant DB calls via shared caching and promise coalescing
- O(1) entity lookups throughout (was O(n))
- Parallel relationship processing (was sequential)
- Cross-agent cache reuse for settings/worlds
- Token usage reduction from data URL summarization and CSV relationship format

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- Safe to merge with minor style improvements recommended
- The PR implements valuable performance optimizations with well-structured caching infrastructure. The code follows good patterns (promise coalescing, timeout protection, TTL management). However, score reduced from 5 to 4 due to: (1) ANSI color code bug in banner.ts that will display wrong colors, (2) reduced type safety by replacing typed interfaces with `any[]` arrays, and (3) missing generic type parameters on parseKeyValueXml calls despite PR description claiming enhancements. These are non-critical issues that don't affect core functionality but should be addressed for code quality. The optimizations themselves are sound and the tests pass (26/29).
- banner.ts (color code bug), reflection.ts (type safety concerns with any[] usage)

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/shared-cache.ts | New comprehensive two-level caching system with agent-specific and cross-agent caches, TTL, in-flight promise tracking, and timeout protection - excellent architecture |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/evaluators/reflection.ts | Restored O(1) Map-based entity lookups (was O(n)), parallel Promise.all() for relationships, and state cache optimization - significant performance improvements |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/index.ts | Added memory creation controls (DISABLE_MEMORY_CREATION, ALLOW_MEMORY_SOURCE_IDS), enhanced data URL handling, action notification handlers, and improved error handling |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/recentMessages.ts | Cross-provider cache reuse, LIMIT_TO_LAST_MESSAGE setting, conditional formatting optimization, entity map for O(1) lookups - substantial efficiency gains |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/entities.ts | Optimized with getCachedRoom/getCachedEntitiesForRoom, O(1) entity Map lookups, efficient component merging |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/settings.ts | Shared cache integration with cross-agent optimization, timeout protection, negative caching for missing settings |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Provider1 as Provider (e.g., entities)
    participant Provider2 as Provider (e.g., recentMessages)
    participant Cache as shared-cache.ts
    participant InFlight as In-Flight Map
    participant DB as Database

    Note over Provider1,DB: Two-Level Caching Strategy

    Provider1->>Cache: getCachedRoom(roomId)
    Cache->>Cache: Check agent-specific cache (by roomId)
    alt Cache Hit (TTL valid)
        Cache-->>Provider1: Return cached room
    else Cache Miss
        Cache->>InFlight: Check in-flight promise
        alt Promise exists
            InFlight-->>Provider1: Return existing promise
        else No promise
            Cache->>DB: runtime.getRoom(roomId) with timeout
            Note over Cache,DB: Timeout: 5 seconds
            DB-->>Cache: Room data
            Cache->>Cache: Store in agent cache
            Cache->>Cache: Store in external cache (source:channelId)
            Cache-->>Provider1: Return room
        end
    end

    Note over Provider2,DB: Cross-Agent Cache Reuse

    Provider2->>Cache: getCachedRoom(same roomId)
    Cache->>Cache: Check agent-specific cache
    Note over Cache: Cache hit! Previous provider<br/>already fetched this room
    Cache-->>Provider2: Return cached room (no DB call)

    Note over Provider1,DB: Cross-Agent External Cache

    Provider1->>Cache: getCachedSettingsByServerId(guildId)
    Cache->>Cache: Check external cache (guild:guildId)
    Note over Cache: Shared across ALL agents<br/>using raw Discord IDs
    alt Cross-Agent Hit
        Cache-->>Provider1: Return settings (no DB call)
    else Miss
        Provider1->>Cache: getCachedWorld(worldId)
        Cache->>DB: Fetch world with timeout
        DB-->>Cache: World with settings
        Cache->>Cache: Store by worldId (agent-specific)
        Cache->>Cache: Store by guildId (cross-agent)
        Cache-->>Provider1: Return world settings
    end

    Note over Provider2,DB: Negative Caching

    Provider2->>Cache: hasNoSettings(serverId)
    Cache->>Cache: Check negative cache
    alt Known No Settings
        Cache-->>Provider2: true (skip DB call)
    else Unknown
        Cache-->>Provider2: false
        Provider2->>DB: Fetch settings
        DB-->>Provider2: No settings found
        Provider2->>Cache: markNoSettings(serverId)
        Note over Cache: Future requests skip DB
    end
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->43:T77c,## Summary

Server-side infrastructure for running Milaidy agents in ELIZA Cloud sandboxes with dedicated Neon PostgreSQL databases, automatic backups, and SSE streaming.

### Database
- `milaidy_sandboxes` table: per-agent sandbox records (status, Neon credentials, Vercel IDs, heartbeat)
- `milaidy_sandbox_backups` table: rolling state snapshots (10 max, auto-pruned)
- Migration 0029 + rollback SQL

### Service: MilaidySandboxService
- **Provision**: Neon DB creation via getNeonClient() + Vercel Sandbox.create() + health check polling + state restore from backup
- **Bridge proxy**: JSON-RPC forwarding to sandbox :18790/bridge
- **SSE streaming**: Proxies upstream SSE body from sandbox /bridge/stream
- **Snapshots**: Fetches state from sandbox, persists to DB, prunes old backups
- **Heartbeat**: Updates last_heartbeat_at, marks disconnected on failure
- **Shutdown**: Pre-shutdown backup + Sandbox.get().shutdown() via SDK

### API Routes (/api/v1/milaidy/agents/*)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|--------|------|---------|
| GET | /agents | List all agents for org |
| POST | /agents | Create agent (Zod validated) |
| GET | /agents/:id | Get agent details |
| DELETE | /agents/:id | Delete with Neon cleanup |
| POST | /agents/:id/provision | Provision sandbox (120s max) |
| POST | /agents/:id/bridge | JSON-RPC proxy |
| POST | /agents/:id/stream | SSE streaming proxy |
| POST | /agents/:id/snapshot | Trigger backup |
| GET | /agents/:id/backups | List backups |
| POST | /agents/:id/restore | Restore from backup |

### Companion PR
- Milaidy client: https://github.com/milady-ai/milaidy/pull/16

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds a robust multi-provider RPC abstraction layer that supports Alchemy (29 chains), Infura (27 chains), Ankr (25 chains), and Eliza Cloud proxy (32 chains). The implementation provides automatic fallback when a preferred provider doesn't support a specific chain, while maintaining backward compatibility with per-chain custom RPC URLs.

**Key changes:**
- New `rpc-providers.ts` module with provider factory pattern and chain mapping
- `EVM_RPC_PROVIDER` setting for provider preference with automatic fallback
- Priority ordering: custom RPC URLs > preferred provider > other providers > viem defaults
- Startup validation with helpful warnings when no provider is configured
- 45 unit tests and 16 live integration tests with 100% pass rate

**Minor issue:**
- Custom RPC URLs are incorrectly labeled as `"alchemy"` provider in logs (line 256 of `rpc-providers.ts`), which could cause confusion during debugging. Consider adding `"custom"` to the `RPCProviderName` type union.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- Safe to merge with one minor logging inconsistency that doesn't affect functionality
- Strong implementation with comprehensive test coverage (61 total tests), proper error handling, and clean architecture. The only issue is cosmetic - custom RPC URLs are mislabeled in logs. No breaking changes, security issues, or logic errors detected.
- typescript/rpc-providers.ts - address the custom RPC provider name labeling issue

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| typescript/rpc-providers.ts | New multi-provider RPC abstraction with support for Alchemy, Infura, Ankr, and Eliza Cloud. Minor issue: custom RPC URLs incorrectly labeled as "alchemy" provider in logs. |
| typescript/providers/wallet.ts | Integrates new RPC provider manager into wallet initialization. Clean refactoring that replaces direct environment variable reads with centralized provider resolution. |
| typescript/__tests__/unit/rpc-providers.test.ts | Comprehensive unit tests covering provider resolution, fallback logic, priority ordering, and edge cases. 45 test cases provide excellent coverage. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Runtime as IAgentRuntime
    participant Wallet as WalletProvider
    participant Manager as RPCProviderManager
    participant Providers as Provider Configs

    Wallet->>Runtime: validateRPCProviderConfig()
    Runtime-->>Wallet: {valid, providers, warnings}
    Wallet->>Manager: initRPCProviderManager(runtime)
    Manager->>Runtime: getSetting("EVM_RPC_PROVIDER")
    Runtime-->>Manager: preferred provider
    Manager->>Runtime: getSetting("ALCHEMY_API_KEY")
    Manager->>Runtime: getSetting("INFURA_API_KEY")
    Manager->>Runtime: getSetting("ANKR_API_KEY")
    Manager->>Runtime: getSetting("ELIZAOS_CLOUD_*")
    Manager->>Providers: create configured providers
    Providers-->>Manager: provider configs array
    Manager->>Manager: sort by preference & chain coverage
    Manager-->>Wallet: RPCProviderManager instance
    
    loop for each chain
        Wallet->>Manager: resolveForChain(chainName)
        Manager->>Runtime: check custom RPC (ETHEREUM_PROVIDER_*)
        alt has custom RPC
            Manager-->>Wallet: {providerName, rpcUrl, headers}
        else no custom RPC
            Manager->>Providers: find first provider supporting chain
            Providers->>Providers: buildUrl(chainName, apiKey)
            Providers-->>Manager: RPC URL
            Manager-->>Wallet: {providerName, rpcUrl, headers}
        end
        Wallet->>Wallet: genChainFromName(chainName, rpcUrl)
    end
```

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->47:T1dcb,## Summary
- Make all HTTP MCP connections lazy after schema fetch (connect → fetch schemas → disconnect → register lazy). `ensureConnected()` creates per-entity connections with correct API keys from request context.
- Add `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` check in `validate()` via `runtime.getSetting()` for per-user OAuth tool filtering. Fail-open when unset for CLI/non-cloud compatibility.
- Remove all `as any` casts; add `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to transport union type.
- Make `getRequestContext` import optional with info/warn logging when unavailable (not in all `@elizaos/core` versions).

## Context
In the multi-tenant cloud environment, a shared `AgentRuntime` is cached per `agentId:orgId`. Previously, HTTP MCP connections were established at init with the first user's API key baked in, and `validate()` returned `true` for all lazy connections regardless of the user's OAuth connections. This allowed User B to invoke MCP tools using User A's OAuth tokens.

## Changes
- `src/service.ts` — HTTP connections now lazy after schema fetch; optional `getRequestContext` import with logging
- `src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts` — `validate()` checks `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` from request context
- `src/types.ts` — `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` added to `McpConnection.transport` union
- `tests/dynamic-tool-actions.test.ts` — 3 new tests for MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS filtering (include, exclude, unset)

## Test plan
- [x] 207 tests pass, 0 fail
- [x] Pre-existing TS errors unchanged (6, all in untouched files)
- [x] Biome clean on all changed files
- [ ] Verify in cloud environment that User A's OAuth tools are not accessible to User B
- [ ] Verify CLI/standalone usage unaffected (MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS unset → all tools available)

## Companion PR
- eliza-cloud-v2: elizaOS/eliza-cloud-v2#315 (cross-instance cache invalidation + request context wiring)

> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Touches core MCP connection lifecycle and action validation logic; mistakes could cause connection churn or incorrectly deny/allow tool access in multi-tenant deployments.
> 
> **Overview**
> **Fixes a multi-tenant MCP isolation issue** by changing HTTP server initialization to connect only long enough to fetch schemas, then disconnect and mark the server as lazy so real tool/resource usage reconnects per request/entity with the correct credentials.
> 
> Adds per-user OAuth gating via new `checkMcpOAuthAccess()` (driven by request-context `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS`) and wires it into dynamic tool action `validate()` and `READ_MCP_RESOURCE` validation, with fail-open behavior when the setting is absent. Also hardens `McpService` with an optional `getRequestContext` dynamic import, per-connection locking to avoid duplicate connects, and transport typing updates (adds `StreamableHTTPClientTransport`), plus accompanying unit tests for the new access checks.
> 
> <sup>Written by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot) for commit 868a2606ea08f6370bf21e0be745c5a23d8a6c2c. This will update automatically on new commits. Configure [here](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot).</sup>

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR addresses a critical multi-tenant security vulnerability where HTTP MCP connections shared state across users, allowing User B to invoke tools with User A's OAuth tokens.

**Key Changes:**
- Made HTTP MCP connections lazy after schema fetch (connect → fetch schemas → disconnect → register lazy) so `ensureConnected()` creates per-user connections with correct credentials from request context
- Added `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` validation check in `validate()` to filter tools based on user's OAuth connections (fail-open when unset for CLI compatibility)
- Removed all `as any` casts by adding `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to the transport union type in `src/types.ts:83`
- Made `getRequestContext` import optional with graceful fallback logging for older `@elizaos/core` versions
- Improved type safety in `getSettings()` by explicitly handling all return types from `getSetting()`

**Tests:**
- Added 3 new tests covering `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` filtering (include, exclude, unset scenarios)
- All 207 tests pass

**Security Impact:**
This PR successfully eliminates the shared-state vulnerability that allowed cross-user OAuth token access in multi-tenant environments.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with one minor issue to address
- Score reflects strong security improvements and comprehensive test coverage, but has one logic issue: missing try-catch around JSON.parse in the MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS validation that could cause validate() to throw instead of returning false on malformed JSON
- src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts needs JSON.parse error handling at line 61

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/service.ts | Implements lazy HTTP connections with per-user isolation using optional getRequestContext. Removes 'as any' casts and adds proper type handling for transport union. |
| src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts | Adds MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS validation check to prevent cross-user OAuth token access. Formatting improvements throughout. |
| src/types.ts | Adds StreamableHTTPClientTransport to McpConnection.transport union type, fixing previous type cast requirement. |
| tests/dynamic-tool-actions.test.ts | Adds 3 new tests for MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS filtering behavior (include, exclude, unset). Updates existing tests to mock getSetting. |

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant UA as User A
    participant UB as User B
    participant Act as MCP Action
    participant RT as Runtime
    participant Svc as McpService
    participant Srv as HTTP Server

    Note over Svc,Srv: Init: HTTP servers become lazy
    Svc->>Srv: Connect & fetch schemas
    Srv-->>Svc: Tools/schemas
    Svc->>Svc: Disconnect & mark lazy
    Svc->>Svc: Register actions

    Note over UA,Srv: User A invokes tool
    UA->>Act: Invoke tool
    Act->>RT: validate()
    RT-->>Act: MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS=["google"]
    Act->>Svc: isLazyConnection("google")?
    Svc-->>Act: true
    Act-->>UA: Valid
    
    Act->>Svc: callTool(google, search)
    Svc->>Svc: ensureConnected(google)
    Svc->>Svc: getRequestContext()->userA
    Svc->>Srv: Connect as google:userA
    Srv-->>Svc: Connected
    Svc->>Srv: Execute tool
    Srv-->>Svc: Result
    Svc-->>UA: Success

    Note over UB,Srv: User B attempts same tool
    UB->>Act: Invoke tool
    Act->>RT: validate()
    RT-->>Act: MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS=["notion"]
    Act->>Act: Check google in list
    Act-->>UB: OAuth check denied
    
    Note over Act: User B blocked from User A tools
```

</ul>
<p><strong>Full Changelog</strong>: <a href="https://github.com/honojs/hono/compare/v4.11.7...v4.11.8">https://github.com/honojs/hono/compare/v4.11.7...v4.11.8</a></p>
<h2>v4.11.7</h2>
<h1>Security Release</h1>
<p>This release includes security fixes for multiple vulnerabilities in Hono and related middleware. We recommend upgrading if you are using any of the affected components.</p>
<h2>Components</h2>
<h3>IP Restriction Middleware</h3>
<p>Fixed an IPv4 address validation bypass that could allow IP-based access control to be bypassed under certain configurations.</p>
<h3>Cache Middleware</h3>
<p>Fixed an issue where responses marked with <code>Cache-Control: private</code> or <code>no-store</code> could be cached, potentially leading to information disclosure on some runtimes.</p>
<h3>Serve Static Middleware (Cloudflare Workers adapter)</h3>
<p>Fixed an issue that could allow unintended access to internal asset keys when serving static files with user-controlled paths.</p>
<h3>hono/jsx <code>ErrorBoundary</code></h3>
<p>Fixed a reflected Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) issue in the <code>ErrorBoundary</code> component that could occur when untrusted strings were rendered without proper escaping.</p>
<h2>Recommendation</h2>
<p>Users are encouraged to upgrade to this release, especially if they:</p>
<ul>
<li>Use IP Restriction Middleware</li>
<li>Use Cache Middleware on Deno, Bun, or Node.js</li>
<li>Use Serve Static Middleware with user-controlled paths on Cloudflare Workers</li>
<li>Render untrusted data inside <code>ErrorBoundary</code> components</li>
</ul>

</blockquote>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

Updated `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` from 1.17.3 to 1.26.0 across 3 directories (main agent, integration tests, and example client), addressing critical security vulnerability GHSA-345p-7cg4-v4c7 where shared server/transport instances could leak cross-client response data.

**Key Changes:**
- Main package upgraded from 1.17.3 → 1.26.0 (security patch addressing response data leakage)
- Example client upgraded from 1.25.1 → 1.26.0 (minor version jump)
- Transitive dependency updates include `hono` 4.11.1 → 4.11.9 (security release fixing XSS, cache middleware, IP restriction, and static file access issues) and `qs` 6.14.0 → 6.14.1 (fixes for array length handling and ReDoS prevention)
- All changes are backwards-compatible within semver caret ranges

**Security Improvements:**
- Fixes critical data leakage vulnerability in MCP SDK
- ReDoS prevention in UriTemplate regex patterns
- Hono security patches for XSS, cache control, IP validation, and asset access
- Improved client sampling validation

The update is standard Dependabot maintenance with important security fixes. Code using the SDK APIs (`Client`, `StdioClientTransport`, `ElicitRequestSchema`, etc.) remains compatible as these are non-breaking updates within the v1.x line.

> [!NOTE]
> Introduces persistent, database-backed caching to reduce duplicate OpenAI calls and latency.
> 
> - `audio.ts` `handleTranscription`: content-based cache keys (slice-hashed blobs + params), best-effort get/set, and detailed debug logs
> - `image.ts` `handleImageDescription`: URL/ETag/Last-Modified-based cache (default prompts only), consolidated `HEAD` request with 2s timeout, and >20MB size rejection with user-facing message
> - Uses `max_completion_tokens` for newer models (`gpt-5`/`o1`/`o3`) while keeping `max_tokens` fallback
> - Improved error parsing/logging with response body capture; cache failures are non-blocking
> - No behavioral change to image generation; TTS helper unchanged aside from plumbing

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR adds persistent database-backed caching to the audio transcription and image description handlers to optimize for Discord bots and other services that frequently process the same media files. The implementation includes:

**Audio transcriptions (`audio.ts`)**:
- Content-based caching using SHA-256 hashing (with fallback) to identify duplicate audio regardless of URL
- Reads audio buffer once for both cache check and API call to avoid redundant I/O
- Best-effort caching that doesn't block requests on cache failures

**Image descriptions (`image.ts`)**:
- URL-based caching for default prompts only (custom prompts bypass cache)
- Image size validation via HEAD request to fail fast on oversized images (>20MB)
- Support for newer model token parameters (`max_completion_tokens` for gpt-5/o1/o3 series)
- Enhanced error handling with detailed error response logging

Both handlers follow a consistent pattern: check cache first, log appropriately, make API call if needed, cache successful results. The caching is designed to be non-intrusive with comprehensive logging for debugging.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk - well-designed caching implementation with proper error handling
- Score reflects solid architecture and error handling, but there's a minor cache collision risk in image.ts where different images at the same URL could be cached incorrectly if image content changes without URL change
- Pay attention to `src/models/image.ts` - the URL-based caching could cause issues if image content at a URL changes without the URL changing

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/models/audio.ts | Added content-based caching for transcriptions with robust SHA-256 hashing and fallback. Well-designed with proper error handling and detailed documentation. |
| src/models/image.ts | Added URL-based caching, size validation, improved error handling, and max_completion_tokens support for newer models. Minor cache collision risk with URL-only keys. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant Handler as Audio/Image Handler
    participant Cache as Database Cache
    participant OpenAI as OpenAI API

    Client->>Handler: Request transcription/description
    Handler->>Handler: Prepare media (hash audio/extract URL)
    Handler->>Cache: Check cache with key
    alt Cache HIT
        Cache-->>Handler: Return cached result
        Handler-->>Client: Return result (fast path)
    else Cache MISS
        Cache-->>Handler: No cached result
        Handler->>OpenAI: API request with media
        OpenAI-->>Handler: API response
        Handler->>Cache: Store result (best-effort)
        alt Cache write succeeds
            Cache-->>Handler: Write confirmed
        else Cache write fails
            Cache-->>Handler: Write failed (logged, non-blocking)
        end
        Handler-->>Client: Return result
    end
```

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR fixes a critical issue where `elizaos create` failed when the CLI was linked from the monorepo because alpha versions (like `1.7.2-alpha.0`) couldn't be found on npm.

## Key Changes

- **Build-time script** (`copy-templates.ts`): Now unconditionally converts `workspace:*` to `'latest'` instead of pinning to CLI version
- **Runtime utility** (`copy-template.ts`): Simplified `normalizeElizaDeps` to always set `@elizaos/*` dependencies to `'latest'`, ensuring npm installability regardless of how templates are bundled
- **Comprehensive tests**: Added integration tests verifying monorepo development, linked CLI usage, bundled template versions, and version consistency

## Impact

Projects created with `elizaos create` will now successfully install dependencies from npm, regardless of whether the CLI is:
- Installed globally from npm
- Linked from the monorepo during development
- Running an alpha/beta version

The approach aligns with the repository's philosophy: monorepo packages use `workspace:*` (per CLAUDE.md guidelines), but generated user projects use `'latest'` for npm compatibility.

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR changes the CLI’s template handling so newly created projects always use `'latest'` for `@elizaos/*` dependencies instead of `workspace:*`/pinned versions, aiming to ensure projects created from the published CLI can always install from npm. It updates both the build-time template bundling script (`packages/cli/src/scripts/copy-templates.ts`) and the runtime template copier (`packages/cli/src/utils/copy-template.ts`), and adjusts CI/test timeouts plus adds local-development/integration coverage.

Key concerns are around correctness of the version-rewrite scope (build script currently rewrites any `workspace:*`, not just `@elizaos/*`) and preserving the intended behavior of `elizaos create --local` (runtime now forces `@elizaos/*` to `'latest'` unconditionally).

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

- This PR is not safe to merge until the new integration test import error and template version-rewrite logic are corrected.
- Score reflects two definite pre-merge issues: (1) `packages/cli/tests/integration/local-development.test.ts` references `__dirname` in ESM, causing an import-time crash, and (2) both build-time and runtime dependency rewriting are broader than intended, likely breaking local/monorepo flows (`--local`) and potentially rewriting non-published workspace deps to `latest`.
- packages/cli/tests/integration/local-development.test.ts, packages/cli/src/utils/copy-template.ts, packages/cli/src/scripts/copy-templates.ts

50:T1018,This PR adds comprehensive documentation for server environment variables, addressing #5716.

## Summary

Added `docs/environment-variables.md` with detailed documentation for:
- `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` - API authentication for securing endpoints
- `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` - Web UI control for security and deployment flexibility

## Content

The documentation includes:
- **Purpose and behavior** for each environment variable
- **Default values** and security implications
- **Usage examples** showing how to configure each variable
- **Deployment scenarios**:
  - Production deployment (secure)
  - Development setup (convenient)
  - Headless API server
- **Related files** references for implementation details

## Why This Matters

Proper documentation of these environment variables is important because:
1. **Security**: `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` controls API access - users need to understand how to secure their deployments
2. **Attack Surface**: `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` allows disabling the UI to reduce exposure in production
3. **Flexibility**: Both variables support different deployment scenarios

Closes #5716

```mermaid
    User->>Express: "GET /api/agents (with X-API-KEY header)"
    Express->>AuthMiddleware: "apiKeyAuthMiddleware()"
    AuthMiddleware->>AuthMiddleware: "Check ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN env"
    alt ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN configured
        AuthMiddleware->>AuthMiddleware: "Validate X-API-KEY header"
        alt Valid API key
            AuthMiddleware->>Express: "next()"
            Express->>AgentServer: "Handle API request"
            AgentServer-->>Express: "Return response"
            Express-->>User: "API response"
        else Invalid/missing API key
            AuthMiddleware-->>User: "401 Unauthorized"
        end
    else ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN not configured
        AuthMiddleware->>Express: "next() - skip auth"
        Express->>AgentServer: "Handle API request"
        AgentServer-->>Express: "Return response"
        Express-->>User: "API response"
    end
```

Related to ART (Agentic Reinforcement Training) example improvements for v2.0.0

# Risks

Low. Changes are isolated to the `examples/art` directory and root `.gitignore`. Only adds new dependencies, enhances existing functionality, and improves test coverage without breaking changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR enhances the ART (Agentic Reinforcement Training) example for the 2048 game with the following improvements:

### Dependencies (`pyproject.toml`)
- Added `peft>=0.7.0` - Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning library
- Added `unsloth>=2024.2` - Fast LLM fine-tuning library  
- Added `trl>=0.7.10` - Transformer Reinforcement Learning library

### Trainer Improvements (`trainer.py`)
- Updated to use `art.TrainableModel` instead of `art.Model`
- Configured `art.local.LocalBackend` for vLLM with proper inference server setup
- Enhanced training pipeline to use `backend._train_model()` directly
- Added OpenAI client for chat completions with configurable temperature

### CLI Enhancements (`cli.py`)
- Added `--rollouts` option for rollouts per group
- Added `--groups` option for groups per step
- Added `--lr` option for learning rate
- Added `--judge` option for RULER judge model

### Game Module Updates (`game_2048/__init__.py`)
- Exported `Game2048HeuristicAgent`
- Exported `Game2048RandomAgent`

### Test Improvements
- Enhanced `test_games.py` with clearer player action logic and improved `test_full_game`
- Updated `test_integration.py` to include `ElizaActionAttempt` in the complete step process for better action tracking

### Documentation (`README.md`)
- Added instructions for setting up a Python virtual environment
- Added alternative command with `HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER=0` flag for troubleshooting

### .gitignore Updates
- Added `.art` and `art_venv` to root `.gitignore`
- Created `examples/art/.gitignore` with `unsloth_compiled_cache/` and `.art`

## What kind of change is this?

Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The README within the `examples/art` directory has already been updated as part of this PR.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `examples/art/pyproject.toml` - Review the new dependencies
2. `examples/art/elizaos_art/trainer.py` - Review the trainer logic changes
3. `examples/art/elizaos_art/games/game_2048/cli.py` - Review the new CLI options
4. `examples/art/tests/` - Review the test improvements

## Detailed testing steps

1. Navigate to the `examples/art` directory
2. Create and activate a virtual environment:
   ```bash
   cd examples/art
   python -m venv venv
   source venv/bin/activate
   ```
3. Install dependencies:
   ```bash
   pip install -e ".[dev,local]"
   ```
4. Run tests:
   ```bash
   pytest tests/
   ```
5. Run the training pipeline:
   ```bash
   elizaos-art-2048 pipeline --steps 50 --rollouts 8
   ```
   Or with the alternative command if the above fails:
   ```bash
   HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER=0 elizaos-art-2048 pipeline --steps 50 --rollouts 8
   ```
